## Document 341: How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/How bad is the rise in bond yields_ (2026-09-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

SPECIAL EDITION | HOW BAS IS THE RISE 
IN BOND YIELDS?

TRANSCRIPTION

0:00:10 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Everyone, welcome. As we head into autumn 2026, interest rates are reaching levels never seen for many, many years. 
Why is this happening? Is it sustainable? And what are the implications for the economies of different countries? All these 
questions will be discussed over the next few minutes with the economists of BNP Paribas. Let us begin with Isabelle 
Mateos y Lago, chief economist of the group. Hello, Isabelle.  
0:00:38 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everyone. 
0:00:39 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you for being with us today. Just to begin, a simple explanation. What rates are we talking about and which countries 
are affected? 
0:00:48 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, it is very important to clarify this. So, we are talking about long-term interest rates, rates at 10 years or longer 
maturities, 30 years, etc. The rates at which sovereign states borrow on the market. And this is primarily something that is 
happening in advanced countries, so United States, United King dom, Japan, and then the Eurozone countries. And indeed, 
as you said, we are seeing since the beginning of the summer, interest rates reach levels we had not seen for about 20 
years. But it is important to keep in mind that up until that point, up until the mid -2000s, the level of interest rates that 
we're seeing now were really quite common, quite normal, so one shouldn't overdramatize.  
0:01:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why do we find ourselves in this situation and how did we get to this point?  
0:01:41 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Yes, so the direct cause is that investors are demanding higher interest rates in order to  lend their capital to governments. 
But why is this? Well, there's essentially three reasons for this, and not all of them are bad, and they're not mutually 
exclusive either. The top reason to my mind is that there's just greater competition for capital be tween sovereign states 
and the corporate sector, who are all facing simultaneously additional investment needs, in defense, in artificial 
intelligence, in energy transition, in resilience. And so,  you just have greater competition for capital and that pushes up the 
price of capital, which is the interest rate. 
Reason number two is because markets have revised upwards their expectations of how high central banks are going to be 
taking their interest rates. 
And this mechanically translates into higher rates at the long end. And then there's a third reason, which doesn't apply 
necessarily in every case, but which is essentially higher sovereign risk, a kind of a risk premium, in cases where there are  
concerns arising about the sustainability of these public debts.

### Page 2

2

0:02:59 - Emmanuel Laborde 
What are the implications for global economies? 
0:03:03 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
So that's exactly what we'll be discussing in the next segments. But basically, the two key issues are, one, an adverse 
impact on public finances. It's higher interest rates making it harder to balance the books in terms of public accounts. And 
then secondly, these higher rates tend to slow down economic growth. Of course, by how much and how serious it will be, 
depends on how high interest rates go and how long they stay there. But that's for the next segments.  
0:03:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Yes. Thank you very much, Isabelle. Having said the scene now, let's move on to the first segment with the following 
question. What makes debt sustainable? And let's see what Hélène Baudchon has to say.  
Hello Hélène and welcome into this special edition. 
0:03:58 - Hélène Baudchon 
Hello Emmanuel, hello everybody. 
0:04:01 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When economists talk about public debt, they often refer to the sustainability of debt. As an economist, can you explain to 
us what that means and what is the debt trajectory deemed unsustainable?  
0:04:15 - Hélène Baudchon 
To begin with, I will say that a temporary increase in debt can be seen as a normal evolution, not particularly concerning, 
for instance during an economic crisis or more recently during a health crisis. In such cases, it acts as a buffer. A problem  
arises when the debt level never comes back down. An unsustainable trajectory can be therefore defined, characterized by 
a continuous regular rise in the debt level, both in absolute terms and as a proportion of GDP, driven by high and recurring 
fiscal deficits. And sometimes interest payments can exacerbate the problem.  
0:05:00 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In very simple terms, you are talking about the snowball effect.  
0:05:03 - Hélène Baudchon 
Exactly explaining why consolidation measures are needed in order to prevent such an adverse situation from occurring 
and in order to prevent, in fact, a sudden loss of confidence from creditors.  
0:05:16 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Regarding governments, what should serve as a warning sign for them? And at what point could the situation become, let's 
say, problematic? 
0:05:27 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, simply speaking, when interest rates rise, not so long ago, up until 2022, Eurozone countries were borrowing at very 
low rates and in some instance, even negative rates. And although debt levels were already at high level at that time, the 
low rates helped manage debt servicing. Not much fiscal effort was needed to keep the public debt ratios in check.  
0:05:55 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Of course, we all remember this because it was a very unusual situation, but now things are very different.

### Page 3

3

0:06:01 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed, the situation today is clearly more challenging simply because bond yields are significantly higher and they 
are rising at the time of our discussion. This is for the part the result of the ECB's monetary tightening in 2022 -2023 aimed 
at addressing the inflation shock then. Then long-term interest rates have risen as a consequence and have aligned more 
closely with GDP growth rate. And in technical terms, this is what we refer to as a narrowing of the "r" - "g" spread. 
0:06:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Why is this spread so important? Why do you, the economists, monitor it this way?  
0:06:48 - Hélène Baudchon 
Essentially, you have the interest rate "r", which determines the debt burden, while the growth rate "g" determines the 
trajectory of fiscal revenues. And if "r" is lower than "g", and this has been the case for many years, as you can see on the  
chart on the screen, it means that interest payments are increasing at a slower pace than the revenue component of the 
equation. 
0:07:14 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this provides some leeway in other budgetary areas, such as education, of course, defense, maybe pensions.  
0:07:22 - Hélène Baudchon 
Yes, indeed. And this is what we refer to as the primary budget. However, if and when interest rates do go up while the 
economy isn't growing that much faster, then fiscal leeway diminishes.  
0:07:35 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Crystal clear. Now, just could you provide a few last words to wrap up the sustainability segment?  
0:07:43 - Hélène Baudchon 
Well, to conclude, we are entering a period which demands greater fiscal discipline or, as some might say, less indiscipline 
in order to manage debt trajectories effectively, in particular in the countries which have high debt levels. And for instanc e, 
the Italian case is an interesting one because it is well known for its high debt level ratio. But in fact, it also has an 
advantageous position because it has no primary deficit. In fact, it even benefits from a large primary surplus.  
0:08:22 - Emmanuel Laborde 
As for the countries... For the other countries... I stop you because this will be a discussion with Guillaume Derrien during  
the next segment. Thank you very much, Hélène. 
0:08:30 - Hélène Baudchon 
Thank you, Emmanuel. 
0:08:36 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Guillaume. Welcome. 
0:08:37 - Guillaume Derrien 
Thank you. 
0:08:38 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Hello, Emmanuel, and hello to everyone. The flexibility of fiscal policy depends heavily on interest rates, which have 
increased considerably over the past few months. The question is, where do we stand today?

### Page 4

4

0:08:52 - Guillaume Derrien 
So, long-term rates have increased in all advanced economies, reaching levels not seen since 2008 in Europe and in the 
United States, and even since 1996 in Japan. Fortunately, the effective rate that states pay on their debt is lower than thos e 
headline figures suggest, simply because the average maturity of debt is relatively long. Precisely how long are we talking 
about? So, it's about 10 years in Japan, 13 years in the United Kingdom, and 8.5 years in France. That means that most of 
the debt was raised before rates started climbing. 
For example, in France, the effective rate just recently rose above the 2% mark, which is significantly lower than the 10 -
year bond yield, which exceeded 4% for new issuances.  
That said, in the United States, which has an average maturity of roughly six years, is experiencing a more rapid increase in  
its effective rate. 
0:09:54 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And this rate increase is happening in the context of high debt -to-GDP ratios and with modest growth, correct? Yes, that's 
correct. 
0:10:05 - Guillaume Derrien 
Debt levels have reached unprecedented heights since the end of World War II. And at the same time, growth is lower, 
hovering around 2% on average in the United States, 1% in Europe and 0.5% in Japan. What does that mean for debt 
servicing costs? So, debt servicing cost is already relatively high, but it is expected to grow further as debt issue before the 
2022 rate hikes mature and are replaced with new instruments bearing higher interest rates. By 2030, interest costs could 
rise to levels last seen in 2000, specifically in France, around 4% of GDP. 
Are all the countries equally affected by these trends? Actually, no. Some countries are managing to reduce their debt 
level. 
Spain, Italy, even Japan have sufficiently improved their primary balance to achieve this, at least for the time being. And o f 
course, we must speak about Germany. Yes, Germany is implementing investment programs that result in a higher public 
deficit and debt-to-GDP ratio. But this ratio started from a relatively low base at around above 60% of GDP. That said, in 
this current context, German bonds have risen. And because German bonds are used as a benchmark for Europe, it  raises 
rates across all Eurozone countries. Is France the only country seeing its public debt go up?  
No, it's not, Emmanuel. This is happening in the United Kingdom and in the United States. These economies, like France, 
are running public deficits that are ever close or exceed 5% of GDP, which is too large for debt ratio to stabilize on its ow n. 
France and the United Kingdom are trying to gradually reduce their public deficit so as not to compromise growth. The 
United States is not going on that path yet, but market pressure could eventually force them to do so.  
0:12:08 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much for your input, Guillaume. Thank you, Emmanuel. Let's go to the next segment. It's about the impact 
of rising bond yields on financing and investments in the Eurozone.  
0:12:26 - Thomas Humblot 
Hello, Thomas. Welcome. Hello. Thank you for having me today.  
0:12:30 - Emmanuel Laborde 
We've just seen what's behind the rise in Eurozone bond yields. This rise is partly attributed to the sovereign spread. What 
impact will this have on the economy? 
0:12:42 - Thomas Humblot 
Actually, this widening of some government bond spreads is obviously not good news, but it tends paradoxically to have 
less impact than an increase in long-term interest rates that does not include the sovereign risk premium. for example, in 
France, for a very long time, it has been considered that the 10 -year OAT was the relevant benchmark for fixed loans. And 
at that time, the spread on French government bonds was relatively stable. But since 2024, that spread increased

### Page 5

5

significantly and that renders the sovereign yield a less relevant benchmark.  
And now the most relevant benchmark is the swap rate because it determines the cost of bank funding that backs new 
loans. 
0:13:56 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Since the end of 2024, we have seen a rise in long-term 10-year swap rates. What impact does this have on bank lending 
rates? 
0:14:08 - Thomas Humblot 
Fixed loans have obviously become more expensive, but to a lesser extent than if they were priced solely based on long -
term sovereign rates. And what about existing fixed-rate loans? 
There is no impact. An increase in rates for the entire maturity of a loan does not have any effect if it is a fixed -rate loan. 
Borrowers with outstanding loans are therefore protected. Obviously, new fixed loans are granted at higher rates. And this 
sharply contrasts with a floating-rates loans. 
Because with floating-rates loans, borrowers experience regular rate adjustments throughout the term of the loan. And in 
terms of bank financing? Strictly in terms of bank financing, an increase in short -term rates has an immediate and 
widespread restrictive impact on borrowers, while for long-term interest rates, the impact is more gradual, and it comes 
gradually as the outstanding amount of fixed-rate loans are progressively renewed. And the thing is that since the 
beginning of the war in Iran, we have seen a combination of the two.  
And in practical terms, the rate of new bank loans to non-financial corporations has increased about 25 basis points in the 
eurozone since the beginning of the year. 
0:16:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Just a few words about companies that borrow directly from the market.  
0:16:16 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, in that case, the impact is even more straightforward. The rate at which a company borrows from the market is the 
risk-free rate plus a premium. 
0:16:28 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Have risk premiums, known as corporate credit spreads, also increased?  
0:16:34 - Thomas Humblot 
Yes, they have increased temporarily at the beginning of the year but have returned to normal since. For the time being, the 
widening remains moderate for the highest-rated investment-grade issuers. 
However, some significant bond issuance in the euro debt market due to some major US technology companies has 
increased spreads for longer maturities. 
0:17:11 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In particular terms, how does this affect the investment decisions of the companies?  
0:17:19 - Thomas Humblot 
Well, investments that were previously worthwhile due to returns sufficient to cover the cost of capital may no longer do so 
and in turn that might lead to a decrease in demand for financing.  
0:17:40 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much Thomas. Now let us move on to the emerging economies. And it is with Cynthia Kalasopatan -Antoine.

### Page 6

6

Hello Cynthia, and welcome. 
0:17:54 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Hi, Emmanuel. Hello, everyone. 
0:17:57 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Long-term bond yields rose significantly in advanced economies over the summer. What can we now say about emerging 
markets? 
0:18:06 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Emerging bond markets held up well, which is quite unusual. The rise in long -term sovereign yields in emerging countries 
has been less pronounced than in advanced economies, including the United States and Germany. Now, there are two 
main exceptions, Turkey and Poland. Meanwhile, we did not witness any significant correction in emerging financial 
markets over the summer, and the main currencies appreciated slightly against the dollar or the euro.  
0:18:37 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Does this mean that public finances are more robust in emerging countries?  
0:18:43 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, it depends. You see, in cases like the Czech Republic and South Korea, public debt is moderate and macroeconomic 
fundamentals are solid. However, these countries are now less frequently considered as emerging and tend to be viewed 
as newly advanced economies. By contrast, Brazil and South Africa have weaker public finances and investors have 
already incorporated a higher sovereign risk into their assessments. And this is reflected in the bond yields. And this 
assessment of sovereign risk did not really change over the summer. 
0:19:26 - Emmanuel Laborde 
When comparing the main fiscal metrics, what are the trends? What do you see as an economist?  
0:19:33 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
On average, the fiscal metrics in emerging markets are not substantially worse than those in advanced economies. The 
average budget deficit levels are similar, ranging from 4% to 5% of GDP in 2025 -2026, according to the IMF. And as in 
advanced economies, the public debt is higher than its pre-Covid level. However, the average public debt to GDP ratio in 
emerging countries excluding China is close to 60%, whereas in advanced economies, it is above 100%.  
0:20:12 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Do you arrive at the same conclusion when comparing debt sustainability criteria?  
0:20:17 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, there are significant regional differences. In emerging Asia, the factors that determine debt sustainability are more 
often favorable than those in advanced economies. In Central Europe, the gap between the interest rate paid on debt and 
the GDP growth rate is also favorable. However, the primary deficits in some countries are high.  
0:20:45 - Emmanuel Laborde 
In a few words, a brief overview of the situation in Latin America.  
0:20:49 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Well, Brazil and Mexico are in a less favorable position. These countries would indeed need to generate a significant 
primary budget surplus each year in order to stabilize their debt in the medium term.

### Page 7

7

0:21:07 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Economists use the term original sin, which refers to the situation when governments resort to borrowing in foreign 
currency to address the shortfall in domestic financing. Does this phenomenon continue to distinguish emerging 
countries? 
0:21:24 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Yes, this is still the case. Emerging countries still have a significant share of their debt in foreign currency. This repres ents 
about 35 to 40% of their total debt on average. And in fact, now, this figure has decreased over the past decade, and this 
contributes to their resilience. However, governments in emerging markets have a less favorable position as regards their 
debt profile and their financing costs are also on average higher.  
0:22:02 - Emmanuel Laborde 
And finally, just a few words about a larger subject, China.  
0:22:07 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
China's public debt is significant and growing rapidly. That being said, it is denominated in domestic currency and issued in  
domestic markets. This debt is, however, sustainable in the medium term due to a very low effective interest rate and a 
high growth rate. 
0:22:29 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Cynthia, for being with us today.  
0:22:32 - Cynthia Kalasopatan-Antoine 
Thank you. 
0:22:33 - Emmanuel Laborde 
It is now time to conclude, and we welcome back Group Chief Economist Isabelle Mateos y Lago.  
It is time for our conclusion with Isabelle. Once again, just a final question, please. Could or should, I do not know which 
word is the good one, central banks intervene to curb this rise in interest rates?  
0:22:58 - Isabelle Mateos y Lago 
Absolutely not. The main contribution that central banks can bring to dealing with this situation is to restore price stabili ty, 
as is their mandate, as promptly as possible. And in the current circumstances, this means keeping relatively high policy 
rates, in some cases, rising them further. And the responsibility for keeping public finances on a sustainable footing rests 
squarely with governments and any attempt by these governments to instead take the easy way out by pressuring central 
banks to lower interest rates prematurely would be enormously counterproductive.  
0:23:41 - Emmanuel Laborde 
Thank you very much, Isabelle. Thank you all for joining us today. Just a few words to remind you that additional economic 
research content can be found by scanning the QR code shown on your screen. Do not hesitate to use it with your 
smartphone. It is very easy. It was a pleasure to be with you today. Have a nice day and hope to see you so

THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 30.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.8%. 
 Of the 13 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 84.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 11.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 11.2%. 
 76.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 65 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 65 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 20.1. 
 During the week of Oct. 5, three S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $773.2B 
from $760.7B). Seven of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the materials (-9.1%) and health care (-2.2%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage decreases 
in earnings, while the energy (16.3%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. Overall, share-
weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 1.6% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the health care (-1.7B) and materials (-1.5B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level decreases in 
earnings, while the information technology (8.3B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 12.5B since the start of the quarter.

earnings above analyst expectations and 7.7% reported earnings below analyst expectations. In a typical quarter 
(since 1994), 67% of companies beat estimates and 20% miss estimates. Over the past four quarters, 82% of 
companies beat the estimates and 15% missed estimates. 
In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 5.3% above estimates, which compares to a long-term (since 
1994) average surprise factor of 4.4% and the average surprise factor over the prior four quarters of 7.7%.

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 30.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.8%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $773.2B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.9B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 504 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer Staples 
sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (114.7%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $55.1B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.7B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (445.6%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (116.1%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 25.1%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (66.5%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $256.1B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $153.8B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (135.6%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (68.9%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 25.3%. 
The Consumer Staples sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $33.7B 
in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $32.6B in 25Q3. 3 of the 10 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower 
earnings than a year ago. The Packaged Foods & Meats (-6.5%) and Household Products (-4.2%) sub-industries have 
the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 6.4%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 31 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, three positive

surprises and five negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.

## Document 391: Sizing Up US Debt

Source PDF: all report pdf/Sizing Up US Debt.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Sizing Up US Debt
Date:    10 minutes ago
Sizing Up US Debt

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Sizing Up US Debt
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 12 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by dostonxd / Unsplash

I. Adding It Up
The bond market had a good day today: It was closed for Columbus Day.
We think that yields have risen high enough to attract buyers. If so, then
yields should stabilize around current levels. Plenty could still go wrong,
making bond yields even more attractive. The war in the Middle East is
likely to heat up again after the midterm elections, sending oil prices

### Page 2

higher. On the other hand, this week's core CPI is likely to rise just 0.2%,
which would likely postpone a Fed rate hike from later this month to early
December.
For now, let's focus on the supply of debt in the US, which is a longer-term
concern for the bond market.
The US Treasury borrowed $749 billion during the three months through
September (chart). That's the change in marketable securities held by the
public over the three months. The 10-year Treasury bond yield rose from

roughly 4.50% to 5.25% over that period. In the past, the supply of
government securities rarely seemed to drive bond yields. The key drivers
were expected and actual inflation and the Fed's reaction to both.
In the past, federal budget deficits tended to widen the most during
recessions, when tax revenues fell faster than the government’s outlays
(chart). The supply of Treasury securities would be increased as the Fed
lowered interest rates to revive economic growth. The deficits would

narrow during economic expansions, as revenues rose faster than
outlays.
Over the past three years, the economy has been booming, but the
government’s budget deficit remains very wide. It hasn't narrowed
because outlays are growing faster than receipts. Allowing that to happen
is fiscal recklessness. That’s because it requires increasing the supply of
government debt during an economic boom, when private credit demands
are surging, which necessarily puts upward pressure on interest rates. If

### Page 3

rates get high enough to cause a credit crunch and a recession, attempts
to revive the economy with deficit-bloating fiscal stimulus could backfire by
pushing bond yields higher. If deficits are this large during good times,
what happens when the bad times arrive?
For now, we believe the backup in US bond yields is driven more by
better-than-expected US economic growth than by the government's
deficit. In our view, bond yields are back to their "old normal," i.e.,

consistent with robust economic growth. In other words, the US isn't on
the edge of a debt crisis. In the Roaring 2020s scenario we expect,
productivity-led economic growth boosts government revenues and lowers
America's deficit and debt ratios relative to nominal GDP. Both Treasury
Secretary Scott Bessent and Fed Chair Kevin Warsh believe this will
happen. The question is whether the Bond Vigilantes will patiently wait for
this outcome. We're not so sure.

For perspective, let's add up US debt and compare it to public and private
debt in other countries:
(1) Total US debt reached a record $118.9 trillion in Q2, including $83.8
trillion in nonfinancial debt and $27.7 trillion in financial debt (chart).

### Page 4

(2) The composition of nonfinancial debt has shifted dramatically since
the Great Financial Crisis (GFC). Before 2008, Treasury debt was smaller
than household and business debt. That relationship reversed as
Washington borrowed heavily during the GFC while the private sector
deleveraged. The Great Virus Crisis (GVC) in 2020 accelerated the shift.
By Q2-2026, marketable Treasury debt held by the public had reached a
record $30.9 trillion, exceeding both household and business debt (chart).

(3) Relative to nominal GDP, total US debt has declined since the GVC
to 374.1% in Q2 (chart). The financial sector drove much of the decline,
with financial debt falling from around 150% to 87.0% of GDP since the
GFC. Nonfinancial debt is at 263.8%, remaining relatively stable in recent
years despite rising government debt, with a brief exception during the
GVC.

### Page 5

That stability masks a significant shift. Treasury debt has surged from
around 30% of GDP before the GFC to 97.2% in Q2, while household
debt has fallen from nearly 100% to 67.0% and business debt has also
declined (chart). Private-sector deleveraging has partly offset rising
government indebtedness relative to GDP.
Separating Treasury debt from total nonfinancial debt makes the
divergence even clearer. Nonfinancial debt excluding Treasuries has fallen

from roughly 200% of GDP during the GFC to 166.5% in Q2, as Treasury
debt surged to 97.2% of GDP (chart).

### Page 6

(4) Total US debt securities and loans reached a record $120.1 trillion in
Q2, comprising $67.3 trillion in securities and $52.7 trillion in loans (chart).
Securities have grown faster than loans since the GFC.
Marketable Treasury securities held by the public have surged to $30.9
trillion, far exceeding corporate and foreign bonds, which they overtook
following the GFC (chart). Government borrowing has become
increasingly dominant in US debt markets.

### Page 7

In short, private-sector debt is down relative to GDP since the GFC, while
the public sector has become more indebted relative to GDP. Healthier
household and business balance sheets should make the economy more
resilient to future shocks, reducing the potential need for government
bailouts and additional borrowing. That could help preserve Treasuries'
traditional role as a safe haven during the next crisis, despite elevated
government debt.
II. Global Comparisons

(1) Total debt/GDP. Japan leads the pack, followed by France, Canada,
and China. The US is roughly in line with the developed country average,
while Germany has the lowest debt ratio among the countries shown
(chart).

### Page 8

(2) Government debt/GDP. Japan also tops the government debt ranking,
followed by Italy and China. The US and France are well above the
developed country average, while Germany stands out with a relatively
low government debt burden (chart).
(3) Private debt/GDP. Canada, France, and China have the highest
private-sector debt ratios, while the US ranks below the developed country
average. Italy has the lowest private-sector debt ratio among the countries

shown (chart).

## Document 393: Strategic product vulnerability_ China least, then Europe and then the US (2026-09-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Strategic product vulnerability_ China least, then Europe and then the US (2026-09-16).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-16
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

16 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
SOURCE: INSTITUTE OF INTERNATIONAL FINANCE, LSEG, MACROBONDCHART
STRATEGIC PRODUCT VULNERABILITY? CHINA LEAST, THEN EUROPE AND THEN THE US
Koen De Leus
“Germany is too dependent on the US for its security, on Russia for its energy and on China for its exports.” That was, in essence, Brookings’
Constanze Stelzenmüller’s diagnosis in June 2022, and it was – and is – valid as well for Europe as a whole.

But does this dependency also apply to technological and industrial products? The European Commission’s EXternal Vulnerability Index
(EXVI) answers that very question, mapping out the EU’s exposure to foreign supply chains.
Relatively good results for the EU
The EU is relatively well positioned, with a score of 0.18 on net-zero technologies and 0.22 on semiconductors. Raw materials appear to be the
weakest point (0.28). Interestingly, compared to its peers, the EU is more vulnerable than China across all three chains (0.13), but significantly

less vulnerable than the US overall (0.22 vs. 0.28). The only sector where the EU score is weaker than the US one is on semi-conductors, and
the difference is not large (0.22 vs 0.19). Like the EU, the US’ vulnerability is biggest in raw material (0.32). This is, among other factors, due to
outsourcing polluting mining starting in the 1990s. Looking at the essential components for the green transition, the EU score on raw materials is
concerning, but the better one on net-zero technologies is more encouraging.

The EU challenge is not its dependencies but its relatively weak global position
Taking a closer look at the two pillars across the different products, it becomes clear that Europe’s problem is mainly related to its global position
(Pillar 2). Dependency (Pillar 1) itself is not the core issue, with much lower scores than the ones for Pillar 2. The EU dependency risk on raw mate-
rials is even comparable to China score (0.24 vs 0.23), and the EU score on semi-conductors (0.13) is the lowest of the other results for the region.

On the other hand, Pillar 2 scores are more negative, illustrating the lack of EU competitiveness in these areas, with scores hovering between 0.29
for net-zero technologies up to a high 0.39 for semiconductors and 0.41 for raw materials. Europe simply does not produce enough of these pro -
ducts at competitive prices, as big European companies in these sectors are limited. Take semiconductors, for example. Imports are not extremely
EXVI is built on two pillars, with two simple questions for each pr oduct: 1/ How dependent is a r egion on a handful of for eign suppliers? 2/ How

globally competitive is it in that pr oduct measured by its imports versus e xports? The result is a single score from 0 (low vulnerability) to 1
(high). A region scores high (vulnerable) when ther e is both a heavy  reliance on others and a weak market position.
Strategic product vulnerability? China least, then Europe and then the US

### Page 2

2
La banque
d’un monde
qui change
Chart of the Week / 16 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
One trend is undeniable: China consistently enjoys the lowest vulnerability. Decades of strategic plans, as well as subsidies and other practices
to restrict open markets, have enabled it to build up domestic supplies and stocks.
The EU’s multiple responses
Looking ahead, the EU can deploy a number of options in response, from circularity to domestic mining, including international partnerships.

Brussels is aiming to mine or recycle 10% of critical materials by 2030, but there is paradox at play here, as circularity only works once sufficient
volumes have first been mined and used. Therefore, in the coming years, international partnerships will be a priority and, for that matter, the EU
has been very active on that front since the start of the second Trump presidency.
The EU is aware of the challenges that it facesAnd the EXVI indices are particularly relevant for assessing supply chains targeted by major policy

initiatives, such as the Chips Act (strengthening the EU’s semiconductor ecosystem and technological sovereignty), the Net Zero Industry Act
(NZIA) (building domestic manufacturing capacity for clean-tech products such as solar, wind and batteries) and the Critical Raw Materials Act
(CRMA) (securing and diversifying the EU’s supply of critical and strategic raw materials). The challenges have been identified and an action plan
has been drawn up. Now it needs to be implemented on the ground.

producing semiconductors itself. That is precisely why the European Chips Act is focusing on building domestic pr oduction capacity rather than

on diversifying imports. The same reasoning applies to raw materials and, to a lesser extent, to net-zero technologies.

Global backdrop

The global landscape remains complex, 
shaped by the interplay of AI, energy 
prices and tariffs, with significant 
implications for growth, inflation and 
financial conditions.

1) AI

The debate over the durability of the AI 
investment boom continues.  Tailwinds 
include technological advances, broader 
adoption, and s trategic and geopolitical 
considerations. Headwinds range from 
uncertainty over investment returns  and 
hyperscaler financing pressure to 
physical bottlenecks , safety concerns, 
and regulatory uncertainty.

Hyperscaler financing pressure has 
attracted increasing attention . In the 
early stages of the AI investment boom, 
hyperscaler capex was largely financed 
by internal cash flow. This is now 
changing, as free cash flow is expected 
to turn negative from 4Q, requiring major 
players to rely on debt issuance and 
other external financing increasingly.

At the same time, higher global interest 
rates are raising the cost of AI 
investment. The Fed resumed rate hikes 
at its September meeting, while global 
bond yields remain under upward 
pressure amid persistent inflation and 
elevated public and private borro wing 
needs. Our baseline assumes US rates 
will rise by 50bps this year and a further 
25bps in 2027 to 4.50%, with global 
long-term rates also remaining under 
pressure. This could increasingly weigh 
on the economics and pace of AI 
investment.

US-China AI competition is also drawing 
increasing attention.  Across the five 
layers of the AI ecosystem, China is 
making rapid progress in energy, 
infrastructure, AI models and 
applications, supported by its large scale, 
cost-effective domestic manufacturing 
supply chains and huge consumer 
market, which provides a wide range of 
real-world scenarios for AI adoption. The 
emergence of a more integrated and 
cost-competitive Chinese AI ecosystem 
could increasingly challe nge the US 
ecosystem, which currently operates at a 
relatively higher cost.

China, however, remains relatively weak 
in semiconductors.  Around half of 
China's domestic AI chip market still 
relies on foreign suppliers, including 
lower-end NVIDIA chips. This 
semiconductor bottleneck remains a key 
constraint on China's AI development. In 
our view, Taiwanese semiconductor 
companies' competiti ve moat therefore 
remains largely intact in the near term.

2) Energy

The US -Iran conflict remains at a 
standoff, keeping the energy -price 
outlook highly uncertain.  New 
developments are likely after the US 
midterm elections in November, with 
both further escalation and de -
escalation possible. Crude oil prices have 
remained elevated at around 
USD100/bbl amid a persistent 
geopolitical risk premium . In contrast, 
refined product prices have risen more 
sharply as refining disruptions and 
tighter product logistics have amplified 
the supply shock. Natural gas prices 
have also increased sharply, reflecting 
disruptions to LNG supply and limited 
alternative shipping routes.

Taiwan has been actively diversifying 
its energy import sources to cushion the 
impact. LNG imports from Qatar have 
fallen to zero since April, while imports 
from the US and other suppliers have 
surged to offset the shortfall, helping 
maintain stable domestic electricity 
supply.

3) Tariffs

US tariff actions are likely to accelerate 
after the US midterm elections in 
November. Two major tariff measures 
remain in the pipeline. The Section 301 
forced-labor tariff was implemented in 
July, while final tariff action under the 
Section 301 overcapacity investigation, 
which covers 16 economies, remains 
pending. Meanwhile, Phase 1 of the 
Section 232 semiconductor tariffs was 
implemented in January, while Phase 2, 
covering a broader range of 
semiconductor-related products, 
remains pending.

Taiwan has secured a relatively 
favourable framework through its trade 
and investment agreements with the 
US. Taiwan signed an Agreement on 
Reciprocal Trade (ART) and an 
Investment MOU with the US earlier this 
year. The ART establishes a 15% US tariff 
rate on Taiwanese goods, while the MOU 
provides Taiwan with preferential 
treatment under Section 232, including 
for semiconductors. The ART has yet to 
enter into force, and it remains to be 
seen how the final Section 301 tariff 
burden will interact with the 15% ART 
framework. Meanwhile, major Taiwanese 
companies such as TSMC are expanding 
their US investments under the 
Investment MOU, which could support 
relatively favourable treatment for 
Taiwan when broader Section 232 
semiconductor tariffs are introduced.

Taiwan 4Q outlook

Taiwan has navigated the complex 
global environment relatively well so far 
this year, supported by robust AI exports 
and investment, diversified energy 
imports, and efforts to absorb higher 
upstream energy costs. Some 
normalisation is likely in 4Q26 –2027, as 
the AI -driven growth boost moderates 
and domestic energy prices face greater 
pressure to adjust.

1) Growth

GDP growth remains broadly on track 
with our expectations. High -frequency 
indicators suggest that GDP growth 
should remain around 12% yoy in 3Q, 
broadly in line with 12.9% in 2Q but below 
the 15.4% peak in 1Q. Growth is expected 
to moderate to a high -single-digit pace 
from 4Q onwards. We maintain our full -
year GDP growth forecasts at 11.6% for 
2026 and 5.6% for 2027.

The AI cycle remains the key driver of 
Taiwan's growth.  Information and 
communication products and electronic 
components accounted for nearly 80% 
of total exports in the first nine months 
of this year, while manufacturing 
accounts for nearly 50% of gross fixed

broadly following the trajectory of 
global hyperscaler capex and 
semiconductor sales. Investment is likely 
to follow a similar path.

For now, Taiwanese technology 
companies continue to report robust 
revenue growth, led by semiconductors, 
ODMs/EMS, and PCs and servers. 
Capacity utilisation at leading Taiwanese 
foundries has also continued to rise, 
suggesting that investment demand 
remains strong.

The AI boom is also beginning to 
generate broader spillovers to the 
domestic economy. The AI-driven equity 
rally has generated significant wealth 
effects, with the TAIEX up 70% year-to-
date and Taiwan becoming the world's 
fifth-largest stock market. Taiwanese 
households have benefited from the 
appreciation of financial assets, with 
equities and mutual funds accounting for 
nearly 30% of total household financial 
assets. The equity rally has also 
generated positive income effects. Wage 
growth has picked up, led by the services 
sector, particularly financial services, as 
strong earnings at financial 
institutions—especially securities 
firms—have prompted higher 
compensation to retain talent. These 
wealth and income gains should support 
household consumption, creating a 
positive feedback loop that broadens the 
benefits of the AI boom across the wider 
economy.

2) Inflation

CPI inflation is also tracking broadly in 
line with our forecast. Headline CPI 
remained elevated at 2.4% yoy in 3Q, up 
from 1.2% in 1Q and 2.2% in 2Q. We 
maintain our forecast for headline CPI 
inflation to remain close to 2.5% in 4Q, 
and retain our full-year forecasts at 2.0% 
for 2026 and 2.1% for 2027.

Energy inflation remains the main 
upside risk.  The government has 
absorbed much of the increase in energy 
costs through state -owned CPC and 
Taipower, limiting the pass -through to 
domestic consumers. With losses at both 
companies widening, the government 
will need to decide whether to provide 
additional subsidies to cover the losses 
or allow further increases in domestic 
energy prices. The September re view 
postponed the adjustment to the 
December meeting, when the overall 
situation will be reassessed after the 
November local elections. Taipower's  
latest review indicated that electricity 
rates would need to rise by 12.8%. A hike 
in electricity prices could lift inflation 
expectations and generate second -
round effects, particularly as the labour 
market tightens and wage pressures 
increase.

3) Monetary policy

The central bank kept the policy 
discount rate unchanged at 2.00% at its 
September meeting, in line with our 
expectations. We continue to expect a 
modest 12.5bp rate hike at the 
December meeting, bringing the policy 
rate to 2.125%.  By then, CPI inflation is 
likely to have remained above 2% for 
around six consecutive months. The 
possibility of an increase in electricity 
prices following the November local 
elections could further lift inflation 
expectations, strengthening the case for 
a December rate hike.

Financial conditions have already 
tightened despite the absence of a 
policy rate hike.  M2 growth remained 
around 7% yoy in 3Q, well below double-
digit nominal GDP growth. At the same 
time, bank loan growth has accelerated 
to more than 10% yoy, driven by strong

corporate credit demand, particularly AI-

and government bond yields.

The central bank has continued to ease 
housing credit controls gradually. At its 
September meeting, it raised the LTV 
cap on second -home mortgage loans to 
70%, the second easing of housing credit 
controls this year. There is scope for 
further easing, including removing the 
LTV cap on second-home loans or raising 
the LTV caps for third - and fourth-home 
loans and high -value properties, which 
currently stand at 30%. However, the 
central bank is likely to proceed 
cautiously, given concerns that a faster 
relaxation could reignite  speculative 
demand and put renewed upward 
pressure on housing prices.

4) Housing

A gradual and modest recovery in the 
housing market is expected from 4Q26 
into 2027.  The existing -home market 
has already shown early signs of 
recovery, with Sinyi  housing prices 
returning to slightly positive yoy growth 
since 2Q. Total building transactions also 
returned to positive yoy growth in 3Q. 
The easing of credit controls should help 
reinforce this modest recovery trend.

The new -home market remains under 
pressure. The number of new units under 
construction has started to decline since 
2023, while new completions are  only 
beginning to ease this year . Demand 
remains weak, as potential buyers 
continue to wait for lower prices. Unsold 
new homes stood at 113K units at end -
2025, compared with a historical normal 
level of around 80K, implying excess 
inventory of roughly 30K units. At the 
current pace of new -home transactions, 
it would take roughly one year to 
normalize the existing excess inventory, 
and longer when factoring in the new 
supply pipeline. Some industry experts 
estimate that it could take as long as 3.8 
years digest current inventories fully 
(here).

## Document 397: The Commodities Feed_ Oil holds firm as Middle East breakthrough remains elusive

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Commodities Feed_ Oil holds firm as Middle East breakthrough remains elusive.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Oil is holding ﬁrm as Middle East tensions refuse to cool.
Meanwhile, IEA members agreed to release the rest of the
emergency stockpiles they pledged earlier this year

Warren Patterson and Ewa Manthey
Energy – IEA member to release remaining
oil
Oil remains well-supported. A pattern has set in: prices slide early in
the session, then claw back most of those losses by the close. As a
result, ICE Brent continues to comfortably trade above $100/bbl.
Uncertainty in the Middle East and concerns over the sustainability
of oil ﬂows through the Strait of Hormuz has the market pricing in a
signiﬁcant risk premium. The longer this pickup in ﬂows continues,
the more likely the market will start giving back some of this risk
premium. In order to see a full removal, there needs to be some kind
of agreement between the US and Iran. This would provide the
market with more conﬁdence that stronger oil ﬂows from the region
will continue.

### Page 2

International Energy Agency members agreed to release the
remaining 100m barrels of oil from strategic reserves. It's part of the
initial 400m barrels that members agreed back in March to help
with the Persian Gulf supply shortfall. According to the IEA,
members have distributed around 330m barrels to date. From the
100m barrels to be released, 20m barrels will be diesel, and will
make its way onto the market as soon as possible. The US probably
won’t be happy with the scale of the release, particularly regarding

diesel. Initial suggestions were that we could see around 50m
barrels of diesel released. The risk is that if the US believes these
volumes are not enough, the administration may mull restrictions
on US diesel exports once again.
The Financial Times reports that the US is considering easing
sanctions on Russia’s shadow tanker ﬂeet in bid to reduce fuel
costs. Tanker freight rates surged to record levels recently due to the
growing ineﬃciency in trade ﬂows out of the Persian Gulf. Since the

start of the Russia-Ukraine war, Russia has built a signiﬁcant ﬂeet of
tankers, which are largely sanctioned. The idea is that easing
sanctions on these vessels would free up tanker capacity for the
broader market. The key question is how eﬀective it might be in
bringing down spot tanker rates. It’s unclear how much of this
capacity is actually available. Given UK and EU sanctions, insurance
could still be an issue.
The latest data from the EIA shows that US commercial crude oil

inventories fell by 8.56m barrels over the last week, which is the
largest weekly decline since February. Hurricane activity in the Gulf
played part in the inventory decline, with oﬀshore production shut
in due to the storm. US output is estimated to have fallen by 300k
b/d week-on-week. Crude oil imports fell by 791k b/d WoW, a key
driver behind the fall in stocks. For reﬁned products, US reﬁneries
increased their utilisation rates by 0.8pp to 93.5%, leaving

utilisation rates at their highest seasonal levels in at least 20 years.
Despite this, distillate fuel stocks still fell by 2.53m barrels over the
last week. This leaves distillate inventories at record seasonal lows
as we head into winter. Meanwhile, gasoline stocks increased by
3.52m barrels.

### Page 3

Metals — Iron ore breaks below $90…
brieﬂy
Iron ore prices fell brieﬂy below $90/t yesterday, the lowest since
November 2022, as weak Chinese demand and abundant global
supply continued to weigh on sentiment. Prices have declined more
than 14% this year, pressured by the prolonged weakness in
China’s property and construction sectors. This is undermining
steel demand. China’s crude steel production during the ﬁrst eight
months of 2026 fell to its lowest level for the period since 2018,

highlighting the impact of the country’s real estate downturn. With
steel mills operating on thin margins, further production cuts
remain possible. Additional supply entering the market has kept
iron ore fundamentals well supplied. As a result, prices are expected
to remain rangebound, with near-term risks skewed to the
downside.
Steel inventories at major Chinese mills rose to 17mt in early
October, up 8.4% from late September, according to CISA data.

Inventories were 6.9% higher than a year earlier, indicating
subdued underlying demand. Meanwhile, crude steel production at
major mills increased 3.1% from late September to 1.94mt/d in
early October.
Agriculture —Cocoa demand recovery
support prices
Cocoa prices ﬁrmed yesterday, with London cocoa settling a little
more than 1% higher amid ﬁrmer demand numbers. Data from the
European Cocoa Association showed European cocoa grindings
rose 3.2% year-on-year and 10% quarter-on-quarter to 348.2kt in

3Q26, marking the ﬁrst annual increase since 2Q24. Similarly, the
Cocoa Association of Asia reported a 29.2% YoY increase in
grindings and a 4.1% QoQ rise to a record 233.8kt.
In its monthly update, the International Grains Council (IGC) raised
its 2026/27 global corn production forecast to 1,307mt from
1,301mt, while consumption was revised up to 1,323mt from
1,321mt. This saw global corn ending stocks increase to 297mt

## Document 431: US MARKET CALL_ Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail

Source PDF: all report pdf/US MARKET CALL_ Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-17
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail
Date:    3 hours ago
US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is
Too Big To Fail
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 17 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Nils Rasmusson / Unsplash
I. Bonds
We've all been suffering from AI fatigue for a while. The daily news about it
has been head-spinning. We've been warned that AI is a bubble that will
burst, just as the late-1990s tech bubble led to the Tech Wreck in the early
2000s. Analogies to the credit excesses of the Great Financial Crisis have

### Page 3

also been proffered. Last week's rout in AI-related stocks convinced the
naysayers that the AI bubble is bursting, just as they’ve long predicted it
would.
Our happier spin is that the Bond Vigilantes are now doing a good job of
regulating and allocating capital to the AI ecosystem. Credit-quality
spreads are widening to reflect the riskiness of the bonds issued by some
of the dodgier AI-related borrowers like CoreWeave and Oracle. The
rapidly rising cost of credit for such borrowers should deflate the AI bubble

without bursting it. If some AI-related credits do blow up, the Godfathers of
the AI mafia (along with their protectors in the government) will clean up
the mess. As in "The Godfather II," Michael Corleone has too much at
stake to let his brother Fredo screw up the family business.
Meanwhile, the rout in the AI corporate bond market has triggered a flight
to quality and safety that has stopped the rout in the US Treasury bond
market (chart). This confirms our thesis that the recent rise in US bond

yields mostly reflects better-than-expected economic growth. Tougher
credit conditions in the AI corporate bond market should curb some of the
AI excesses. However, we doubt this will trigger an economy-wide credit
crunch or a recession. We aren't convinced that it will slow capital
spending much, since labor and materials shortages, along with public
NIMBY pushback, have already slowed the pace of data center
construction.
II. S&P Earnings

### Page 4

The big banks reported mixed but solid overall Q3 earnings last week. On
average, they met banking industry analysts' expectations. Last week, the
consensus EPS for all analysts covering S&P 500 companies edged up to
$365.70 this year, $424.19 next year, and $492.62 in 2028 (chart). Those
are extremely bullish expectations considering that we are still forecasting
$500 in 2030! If the analysts are right, the S&P 500 could rise to 10,000
by the end of next year, assuming a forward P/E of 20.0-21.0. For now, we

are sticking with 10,000 by the end of the Roaring 2020s, i.e., at the end
of 2029.
Last week, Q3's consensus EPS growth rate remained at 24.0% y/y, while
the Q4 estimate rose to 29.8% (chart). We've been among the most
bullish investment strategists on the Street, but analysts are still ahead of
us.

### Page 5

The S&P 500, S&P 400, and S&P 600 continued to rise to record highs
last week (chart).
III. Stock Valuation
The recent stock market selloff has been driven by falling forward P/Es
even as forward earnings have been rising to record highs (chart). The
former are nearing the troughs they made during last year's "Liberation
Day" lows.
Since roughly mid-2025, rising FEMO (fabulous earnings momentum) has
more than offset falling FOMO (fear of missing out) (chart). Since October

6, the reverse has been true: Falling FOMO is outpacing the rise in
FEMO.

### Page 6

IV. Performance
The stock market had a tough week last week. Leading the 1.95%
pullback was MAGS (down 3.89%), while XMAGS fell 1.38% (chart). The
Russell 2000 fell 2.4%, while the Nasdaq fell 4.5%. The DJIA rose 0.67%
for the week. The Impressive 493 is still outperforming the Magnificent-7
on a year-to-date basis, with a gain of 14.1% versus 10.2%.
V. Sentiment
The market letter writers have been more bullish than retail investors
lately (chart). Odds are they will both be a bit more pessimistic this week.

### Page 7

Only 47.4% of the S&P 500 are trading above their 200-day moving
averages (chart).
VI. Commodities
Aluminum prices are falling after matching their 2022 peak. This suggests
that supply-chain disruptions attributable to Gulf War III are receding, as
they had done after Russia invaded Ukraine (chart).

### Page 8

Copper prices remained near their recent record high last week despite
chatter that tightening credit conditions could slow the AI buildout and
weigh on overall GDP growth (chart).
The outlook for higher-for-longer diesel prices offers no relief (chart).

### Page 9

We aren't giving up on gold yet. The current correction resembles
previous ones that occurred in the middle of significant gold bull runs
(chart). This time, rising interest rates have been weighing on the metal's
price. However, significant buying by the People's Bank of China and
other central banks isn't interest-rate sensitive. We are still aiming for a
gold price of $5,000 an ounce by the end of this year or early next year.
💡

log in to the Yardeni QuickTakes website and post them at the end
of the QuickTakes article. Paid members' contributions may be
featured in our segment, "Ed Answers Your Questions".

### Page 10

達發 ESG 評等

電子製造業 ESG 評等分配表

資料來源:臺灣指數公司，國泰證期研究部整理

達發 6526 TT  ( 電子–半導體 ) 
由網入光，發光發熱 
投資與評價 
1. 達發 3Q26 受惠光通放量與商務耳機出貨暢旺，營收創單季新高；
4Q26 光通持續成長，並同步推進 100G 量產與 OOB 新應用導入，營
運為近五年同期新高。 
2. 單通道 200G SerDes 與 25G PON 持續推進，50G/100G 光通、10G 
PON 與 OOB 新應用同步放量，高價值產品占比提升帶動產品組合優
化，預估 26 年 EPS 20.95 元；27 年 EPS 28.36 元，營運創歷史新高。 
3. 目前達發股價為 783 元，約 28 倍 FY27E EPS，位於近四年本益比參考
區間 20-50 倍中間偏下位置。考量光通進入高速成長期，OOB 帶動
AIDC 新應用，轉型方向明確，初次評等給予買進，以近四年本益比參
考區間平均值 35 倍作為評價基礎，目標價 995 元(35x FY27E PER)。

重點更新 
光通與商務耳機出貨暢旺，預期達發 3Q26 營運創近四年單季新高 
達發 3Q26 光通單通道 50G 受惠替換需求與新增標案持續放量 ， 乙太網受惠
低軌衛星規格升級，2.5G PHY 與交換器晶片出貨成長，藍牙則進入消費旺
季，商務耳機出貨增加，營收季增 10.4%至 69.41 億元，創單季新高。毛利
率受記憶體、NOR Flash 及封測成本上升影響，預估季減 2.1 個百分點至
49.1%，營業費用 23.35 億元 （+2.7% QoQ），營業利益 10.73 億元(+13.8% 
QoQ)；預估稅後淨利季增 11.1%至 9.83 億元，EPS 5.87 元，營運創近四年
單季新高。

光通逐季放量，達發 4Q26 營運為近五年同期新高 
達發 4Q26 進入季節性淡季，惟光通逐季放量，預期單通道 100G 逐步進入
量產，乙太網新一代 10G PHY 亦開始出貨，預估營收 64.40 億元（-7.2% 
QoQ） ，季減幅度小於往年。毛利率受上游成本持續上漲影響，惟受惠高毛利
光通持續放量， 預估毛利率持平為 49.1%，營業費用 22.05 億元（-5.6% 
QoQ），營業利益 9.57 億元（-10.8% QoQ）；預估稅後淨利 8.99 億元（-
8.56% QoQ），EPS 5.36 元，營運為近五年同期新高。

鍾沛寰 / IC 設計 
+886-2-2326-9899#1750/ parisphchung@cathayfut.com.tw

### Page 2

訪談內容 
光通與商務耳機出貨暢旺，預期達發 3Q26 營運創近四年單季新高 
達發 3Q26 藍牙進入消費旺季，商務耳機專案持續出貨，高階應用占比提升，惟上半年提前備貨效應逐步消退，藍
牙音訊出貨成長幅度相對溫和。光通方面，單通道 50G 受惠替換需求與新增標案持續放量，大型 CSP 替換專案亦
於下半年開始出貨，搭配出貨的 TIA 搭載率同步提升；乙太網受惠低軌衛星客戶由 1G 升級至 ASP 較高的 2.5G 
PHY，交換器晶片出貨持續成長；寬頻客戶下單節奏則回歸終端需求，3Q26 營收季增 10.4%至 69.41 億元，創單
季新高。 
毛利率方面，記憶體、NOR Flash 及封測成本上升，而產品漲價尚未反映，惟高毛利光通占比提升，部分抵銷成本
壓力，預估毛利率季減 2.1 個百分點至 49.1%。營業費用 23.35 億元（+2.7% QoQ） ，營收規模擴大帶動營業利益
10.73 億元（+13.8% QoQ） ；預估稅後淨利季增11.1%至 9.83 億元，EPS 5.87 元，營運創近四年單季新高。

圖一 : Airoha Revenue and Gross Margin Trend Forecast, 1Q25~4Q27 
資料來源:達發，國泰證期研究部整理

光通逐季放量，預期達發 4Q26 營運為近五年同期新高 
達發 4Q26 藍牙音訊進入季節性淡季，惟產品漲價預期於 1Q27 逐步反映，部分客戶提前於 4Q26 備貨，藍牙出貨
衰退幅度可望優於往年 。 光通方面 ， 單通道50G 延續替換需求 ，100G 預期於 4Q26 完成客戶驗證後逐步進入量產 ，
光通營收持續逐季成長；乙太網新一代 10G PHY 於 2H26 量產，並隨 BMC 出貨切入 AI 資料中心 OOB 應用，預
估 4Q26 營收 64.40 億元（-7.2% QoQ），較 4Q25 衰減幅度大幅縮小，淡季不淡。 
毛利率方面，封測價格持續調漲，產品漲價要到 1Q27 才逐步反映，惟高毛利光通占比持續提升，抵銷成本上漲壓
力，預估毛利率持平於49.1%。營業費用 22.05 億元 （-5.6% QoQ） ，營業利益9.57 億元 （-10.8% QoQ，+100.6% 
YoY） ，預估稅後淨利8.99 億元（-8.6% QoQ，+68.8% YoY），EPS 5.36 元，營運為近五年同期新高。

### Page 3

光通營收大幅成長，預期達發 26 年營運創近五年新高 
達發 26 年光通營收受惠可插拔式光收發模組需求增加、客戶規模擴大及替換需求，預期成長至25 年的四倍以上，
占整體營收比重達 mid-single digit， 其中單通道50G 為主要貢獻 ， 搭配出貨的TIA 搭載率亦持續提升 。 寬頻方面 ，
歐美運營商規格升級帶動 10G PON 預估占寬頻營收比重接近三成，10G PON ASP 約為 G-PON 的兩倍，推升產
品組合優化，並切入 AI 資料中心 OOB 架構；乙太網受惠低軌衛星客戶由 1G 升級至 ASP 較高的 2.5G PHY，加上
交換器市占提升，為成長僅次於光通的產品線；藍牙音訊受惠商務耳機專案落地，高階應用占比提升，預估 26 年
營收年增 20.1%至 251.26 億元，創歷史新高。 
毛利率方面，記憶體、NOR Flash 及封測成本上漲壓抑下半年毛利率，惟光通、10G PON 及商務耳機等高價值產
品占比提升，部分抵銷成本壓力，預估 26 年毛利率年減 1.5 個百分點至 50.4%。營業費用 88.75 億元（+11.5% 
YoY） ，營收規模擴大帶動費用率由25 年的 38.0%降至 35.3%，營業利益 37.90 億元（+30.3% YoY） ；預估稅後淨
利年增 24.4%至 35.16 億元，EPS 20.95 元，營運創近五年新高。

圖二 : Airoha Revenue and Gross Margin Trend Forecast, 2022~2027

資料來源:達發，國泰證期研究部整理

預期 100G 擴大放量，達發 27 年營運為歷史新高 
達發 27 年光通動能由單通道 100G 接棒，100G 已於多家模組廠開案，800G 方案達到指標客戶低功耗要求，預期
27 年逐季放量，100G DSP ASP預估約為 50G 的兩倍；單通道50G 受惠替換需求維持穩健成長，100G 搭配的 TIA
搭載率預期高於 50G，並開始單獨出貨搭配其他供應商的 DSP。預期 27 年光通營收較 26 年再成長三倍以上，占
營收比重提升至一成五左右 。 寬頻方面 ，25G PON 預期於 27 年量產 ，OOB 架構市場規模於 26 至 28 年逐年翻倍 ，
預估資料中心 PON 設備市場 26 至 30 年 CAGR 達 52%；乙太網新一代 10G PHY 貢獻提升，2.5G 交換器亦將放
量，預估 27 年營收年增 17.9%至 296.21 億元，創歷史新高。 
毛利率方面，產品價格結構調整逐步反映成本變動，加上高毛利光通占比提升，預估 27 年毛利率年增 0.6 個百分
點至 51.0%。研發費用隨 100G、200G SerDes 及 25G PON 等新產品持續投入，惟 200G SerDes IP 授權金認列
為無形資產並分期攤銷，對費用影響有限，預估營業費用 98.51 億元（+11.0% YoY） ，費用率降至33.3%，營業利
益 52.49 億元（+38.5% YoY） ；預估稅後淨利年增35.3%至 47.60 億元，EPS 28.36 元，營運創歷史新高。

### Page 4

光通與 OOB 切入 AI 資料中心，達發成長動能升溫 
達發 3Q26 受惠光通放量與商務耳機出貨暢旺，預期營運創近四年單季新高；4Q26 光通持續成長，營運為近五年
同期新高。26 年光通營收預期成長至 25 年的四倍以上，10G PON 占寬頻營收比重接近三成，並與乙太網 PHY 同
步切入 AI 資料中心 OOB 架構；27 年單通道 100G 接棒放量，25G PON 與新一代 10G PHY 開始貢獻，產品應用
由消費性電子與家用寬頻逐步延伸至 AI 資料中心光通訊及帶外管理，預估 26 年及 27 年 EPS 分別為 20.95 元及
28.36 元，成長動能明確，初次評等給予買進投資建議。 
目前達發股價為 783 元(28X FY27E EPS)，位於近四年本益比參考區間 20-50 倍中間偏下位置。考量光通進入高速
成長期，預期 27 年營收占比提升至一成五，100G DSP ASP 約為 50G 的兩倍，高毛利產品占比提高帶動 27 年營
業利益年增 38.5%；加上 OOB 架構市場規模預期於 26 至 28 年逐年翻倍，資料中心 PON 設備市場 26 至 30 年
CAGR 預估達 52%，寬頻與乙太網同步受惠 AI 資料中心建置，產品組合轉型方向明確，以近四年本益比參考區間
平均值 35 倍作為評價基礎，目標價 995 元(35x FY27E EPS)。

產業概況 
AI 資料中心頻寬倍增，PAM4 DSP 隨單通道速率升級 
可插拔式光收發模組為 AI 資料中心 Scale-out 網路中伺服器與交換器間資料傳輸的主要介面 ， 模組內由PAM4 DSP
負責訊號等化與時脈回復，TIA 將光檢測器輸出的電流訊號轉換為電壓訊號，Driver 則負責驅動雷射。PAM4 調變
每個符號承載 2 位元，使單通道傳輸速率由 50G 逐步升級至 100G 及 200G，對應光模組容量由 200G、400G 提
升至 800G 與 1.6T。隨 GPU 叢集規模擴大與交換器頻寬倍增，2025 年 800G PAM4晶片組銷售額成長逾一倍，預
估 1.6T 晶片組 2026 年銷售額將超過 20 億美元。 
不同於過往世代全面切換，800G 與 1.6T 預期將並行部署，加上既有 AI 資料中心光模組逐步進入替換期，單通道
50G 與 100G 需求仍具支撐。晶片價值隨速率提升，單通道 100G DSP ASP 預估約為 50G 的兩倍；惟 2027 年後
線性驅動(LPO)與共封裝光學(CPO)滲透率提升，PAM4 DSP成長斜率預期趨緩，而近封裝光學(NPO)若光引擎不含
DSP，訊號補償將更仰賴 TIA 與 Driver，提升高速類比晶片的重要性。

圖三 : 光收發模組與主動式線材 IC 晶片組銷售預估，2025~2031F 
資料來源:LightCounting，國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 5

PON 世代升級加速，OOB 開啟資料中心新應用 
被動光纖網路(PON)以光纖一對多傳輸，由局端 OLT 經無源分光器連接多個終端 ONU，為光纖到府的主流技術。
規格由 G-PON(下行 2.5Gbps)升級至 10G PON，並朝 25G PON 發展，網通世代轉換週期約五至七年；10G PON 
ASP 預估約為 G-PON 的兩倍，隨歐美運營商規格升級與東南亞滲透率提升，帶動 PON 晶片價值增加。 
AI 資料中心除負責運算的主網路外，另需獨立的帶外管理(OOB)網路監控伺服器狀態，以確保運算不中斷。傳統架
構需於每個機櫃頂端安裝管理型以太網交換器，並以銅線一對一連接伺服器，占用 1 至數個機櫃空間單位(RU)；導
入 PON 後，以免供電的無源分光器取代交換器，依實測數據可節省高達 99%的管理機櫃空間，並精簡布線與降低
功耗，讓機櫃容納更多運算節點。超大型雲端業者已陸續導入，預估資料中心PON 設備市場 26 至 30 年 CAGR 達
52%，累計支出逾 30 億美元；伺服器內 BMC 亦需以太網 PHY 傳輸管理訊號，帶動 PON 與 PHY 同步受惠。

圖四 : 超大型雲端業者資料中心 PON-based OOB 架構 
資料來源:Meta，國泰證期研究部整理

圖五 : 全球資料中心 OOB 管理用 PON 設備營收預估 
資料來源:Dell'Oro Group，國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 6

公司簡介 
達發科技股份有限公司(Airoha Technology Corp., 6526 TT)成立於 2001 年，總部位於新竹縣竹北市，為聯發科集
團旗下 IC 設計公司。2021 年整併誠致科技、雷凌科技、絡達科技及聯發科IoT 部門並更名為達發科技，2022 年 6
月登錄興櫃，2023 年 10 月於證交所上市。公司業務分為有線網通基礎建設與無線邊緣 AI 兩大事業群：有線網通
涵蓋固網寬頻(G-PON、10G PON)、乙太網(PHY 與交換器晶片)及光通訊(PAM4 DSP 與 TIA)，應用由家用寬頻、
低軌衛星地面設備延伸至 AI 資料中心光通訊與帶外管理，並於 2024 年 7 月完成參與九暘私募，九暘為合併子公
司，布局 PoE 晶片；無線邊緣 AI 以藍牙音訊為主，聚焦商務、電競及聲學等中高階耳機市場，並延伸至藍牙傳輸
(電競鍵鼠、電子貨架標籤)及衛星定位(GNSS)晶片。預估 26 年有線網通與無線邊緣 AI 營收占比約各半，其中藍牙
約 40%、固網寬頻約 30%、乙太網約 14%、衛星定位約 10%、光通約 6%。

圖六 : 2026E 產品營收占比預估

資料來源:國泰證期研究部預估

### Page 7

ESG 政策 
達發以社會、經濟、環境及資訊揭露四大構面推動永續經營，已在員工照護、職業安全、董事會獨立性、資
訊安全及溫室氣體管理等面向建立相關制度，評估期間未發生重大人權、消費者權益、職業災害、誠信經營
及環境法規違規情事。

社會面：達發依循國際人權宣言制定人權政策並揭露執行結果，並將人權要求納入供應商評鑑與稽核。員工
管理方面，提供優於法規的休假、保險及身心健康措施，並以員工認股權與持股信託強化人才留任；職業安
全衛生管理系統已取得外部認證，並揭露工傷統計及減災目標。另建立績效評估及能力發展計畫，設定員工
多元化目標、揭露女性主管占比，並足額進用身心障礙員工。未來可完善人權盡職調查流程，並揭露員工滿
意度、訓練成效、性別薪酬比及客戶滿意度。

經濟面：達發每年召開 2 次以上法人說明會，董事長親自出席股東常會，並揭露議案表決結果。獨立董事席
次達半數以上，設置永續發展相關委員會並由董事長擔任最高負責人，且定期執行董事會外部績效評估。另
制定反貪腐、反競爭、內線交易防範及檢舉制度，風險管理政策經董事會通過，並設置資安專責單位、定期
測試資安機制。後續可強化接班規劃、設置提名委員會、提高任一性別董事席次至三分之一以上，並將永續
績效納入高階主管與董事薪酬。

環境面：氣候管理由董事會及管理階層監督，已揭露範疇一、二及範疇三排放，並設定量化中長期減碳、節
能及再生能源目標。達發亦制定水資源及廢棄物管理政策，揭露用水量及有害廢棄物產出量，並將環境表現
納入供應商遴選及稽核。惟尚未取得 ISO 14001 及碳盤查認證，未來可補充 1.5°C 以下及 3°C 以上氣候情境
分析、再生能源使用量及用水、廢棄物量化減量目標。

揭露面：達發依循 GRI 準則編製永續報告書，揭露重大性分析及 SDGs 對應作為，並提供英文版報告書及英
文網站。後續可導入 SASB 對應揭露及第三方確信，建立利害關係人回饋機制，並採用雙重重大性原則強化
ESG 資訊完整性。

主題報告概述 : 本篇報告將分析 2026年美元現況 ，最終形成
2027年美元看法。以下為本篇報告內容概要:
(1) 匯市現況：美國伊朗戰爭導致通膨疑慮再起，美元走強。
(2) 政治議題：2026年共和黨失去絕對執政對美元的影響。
(3) 期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰爭。
(4) 美元基本面：景氣與通膨循環將維持在金髮女孩經濟環境。
(5) 美元技術面：技術面跌破年線，籌碼面偏多但8月轉弱。
(6) 非美匯市: 歐洲政治面利空及日本阻貶為美元提供底部支撐
匯市結論: 美元已從2026年初的降息交易轉向受到美伊戰爭 、

油價上漲及通膨預期升溫支撐 ，但此波強勢更多來自地緣政治
與高利率環境 ，而非美國基本面出現結構性轉強 。期中選舉後 ，
共和黨失去完全執政優勢 ，國會分裂將限制大規模財政擴張 ，
有助抑制通膨與赤字惡化 ，但也降低進一步刺激經濟成長的動
能。展望2027年，美國仍面臨債務上限 、財政協商、貿易政策、
美伊衝突及烏俄戰爭等風險 ，其中地緣政治與油價走勢仍是左
右美元的重要變數 。2027年美元並非進入新一輪強勢多頭週期 ，
而是在高利率支撐下維持區間整理 。2027年美國經濟基準情境
穩健，景氣溫和成長 ，通膨從高檔放緩 。若無重大地緣政治升
級事件，美元指數預期維持97–105區間震盪，並呈現「上有壓
力、下有支撐 」的盤整格局 。當地緣政治升溫時美元偏強 ，當
通膨回落與利率下降時美元偏弱。
資料來源： 中國信託商業銀行整理。2026/9

時性和正確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之
需求及投資之風險，並就投資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不
涉及特定投資標的之建議。
(1)匯市現況 : 美伊戰爭導致通膨疑慮再起，
美元走強
匯市現況: 2026年美元走勢主要受到美國經濟基本面、Fed貨幣
政策預期、川普政策以及地緣政治風險交互影響。
2026年初，川普重返貿易壁壘政策 、美國就業動能放緩且通膨
趨穩，市場普遍預期降息循環延續 ，使美元維持偏弱整理格局 ，
美元指數於1月27日一度跌至全年低點95.55。期間宣布華許出
任Fed主席的消息曾短暫帶動美元反彈 ，但整體仍未擺脫區間
震盪格局 。然而，2月28日伊朗事件爆發 ，3月9日西德州原油
一度接近每桶 120美元，能源價格上升帶動全球通膨預期升溫 ，
美國降息預期下降。進入3-4月，美國經濟數據進一步展現韌性，
製造業 、消費及投資表現改善 ，Fed季度預測亦同步上修 GDP

成長與通膨展望 。受此影響 ，利率期貨隱含年底前降息預期由
原先降息2碼逐步修正至不降息 ，推動美元於2Q26展開反彈。
同時 ，受油價飆升影響 ，9月Fed季度預測將今年 PCE上修至
3.7%，官員點陣圖亦上修利率路徑 ，市場開始討論通膨壓力可
能迫使Fed重新升息 。匯市焦點亦逐漸轉向新任聯準會主席華
許提出的新貨幣政策框架 。在Fed政策預期持續調整下 ，美元
指數於6月24日升至全年高點101.8，隨後進入高檔盤整格局。

(2)政治議題：2026年共和黨失去絕對執政對
美元的影響(2/3)
數席次機率下行趨勢可見 ，當川普政策面無法政治上快速解決
問題並且影響民生時，取得參眾議院的機率加速下行。
期中選舉結果對美元影響 ：觀察1970年以來美國期中選舉結束
後一年美元之平均表現。
民主黨主導兩院 ：當民主黨期中選舉後在參眾議院均為多數 ，
美元指數平均下跌 -4.6%，6次有4次為下跌 ，且前一年美元均
下跌。1971、1987及2007年為下跌幅度最大的3年。
1971年尼克森(共和黨)總統宣布停止黃金兌換 ，10%進口附加
關稅，迫使盟友重啟貿易與貨幣談判(最終達成史密松寧協定)，
美元下跌-7.8%。

1987年雷根(共和黨)延續1985年廣場協議後美元貶值趨勢延續 。
雙赤字壓力 、通膨升溫 、利率上升以及外資拋售壓力導致發生
1987年10月19日黑色星期一股市崩盤 ，美元指數當年下跌 -
17.6%(1986年下跌-16.1%)。
2007年布希(共和黨)總統時期面臨雙赤字 、非美利差縮小 ，以
及次貸風暴風險升溫。美元下跌-8.3%(2006年下跌-8.2%)。

Thematic Report
重點提示
(2)政治議題：2026年共和黨失去絕對執政對
美元的影響(3/3)
共和黨、民主黨各主導一議院 ：當美國國會進入兩黨各主導一
議院時期，美元指數期中選舉過後一年平均上漲 3.6%，4次有3
次為上漲，且前一年4次美元均為上漲。美國期中選舉若造成國
會由不同政黨掌控 ，通常會限制總統隨意推動大規模擴張性財
政政策與赤字支出 。這類財政節制有助於降低通膨失控風險 ，
進而支撐美元走強。4次國會分裂時段其中較大美元升值趨勢為
1983年雷根(共和黨)時期。 1980年以來聯準會主席保羅伏克爾
採取強力升息手段打擊通膨 ，美國利率保持在相對高檔一段時
間且通膨已逐步受到控制 (從1980年15%降至4%以下)。美元

1983年升值14.9%(1982年升值12.1%)。1983年後，美國經濟
順利擺脫先前的嚴重衰退 ，展開強勁增長 ，市場對美國資產信
心大增，形成所謂強勢美元格局 。美元指數在80年代高點轉折
時期美國實質利率(基準利率-消費者物價指數)已從高檔下降。
小結：2026年期中選舉結果可能形成國會分裂 ，國會分裂長期
有助抑制赤字與通膨 ，因此長期對美元有利 ，但短期可能因預
算協商及債務上限僵局增加政治風險 ，對美元造成波動。2027
年將再度面臨美國債務上限 ，以及美伊地緣政治 、貿易戰的變
數均將牽動實質利率的變化 ，因此接下來將進一步分析選後影
響美元的關鍵因子。
✓

對美元有利
✓

隨美元趨勢走弱
✓

Thematic Report
重點提示
(3)期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰
爭(1/3)
期中選舉過後影響美元的關鍵因子聚焦 財政面 (政府關門事件)、
貿易戰(法院裁定關稅無效後宣布以其他方式課徵關稅)、地緣政
治面(伊朗戰爭觸發油價上揚)。
財政面：8月國會通過持續撥款決議至 12月11日，代表政府關
門的風險將落在11月3日後，但新國會於2027年1月3日任職，
因此很可能選後再延後撥款決議 。除此之外 ，根據兩黨政策中
心預測，2027年9月後可能再度面臨美國債務上限議題 ，屆時
面臨2028年預算審核，參眾議院的分治恐延長達成共識的時間
以及提升美國主權債信評或展望調降風險 。從全國納稅人聯盟

(NTU)情境分析預測，美國聯邦政府收支很可能在2027年2月至
7月觸及債務上限。一旦債務達到法定限額，財政部長將宣布暫
停發行債務期 。在暫停發行債務期間內 ，財政部不得增加受法
定限額約束的債務 ，但到期債務仍可展期 。計算美國財政部透
過動用收入 、現有現金儲備和採取非常規措施 ，真正無法履行
聯邦義務約在2028年2月。然而債務上限和債務違約日(X-Date)
何時達到存在很大的不確定性 。受債務上限約束的債務將於
2027年3月接近法定上限 500億美元以內，之後隨著4月稅收的
到來而暫時下降 ，但若政府財政赤字增加超過原先預測 ，X-
Date將會提前到來。

Thematic Report
重點提示
(3)期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰
爭(2/3)
美國信評方面 ，惠譽8月將美國信評維持在 AA+評級且展望穩
定，但警告信評下調風險因子如下 ：政府債務佔GDP水準或償
債能力惡化 ，例如未能解決公共支出和收入的平衡 ，或遭受宏
觀金融衝擊 。經濟政策制定可信度下降導致削弱美元儲備貨幣
地位，從而降低政府融資彈性 。惠譽預期 ，美國債務占GDP比
重到2028年可能升至123%，較AA評級國家46.3%的債務比率
中值高出一倍以上 ，因此，美國信評或展望下修的風險有可能
同時伴隨財政惡化或經濟成長放緩 /衰退造成償債能力惡化 。
2027年美國財政議題將繼續面臨逆風 ，共和黨失去完全執政的

環境對財政有利但政治僵局則凸顯債務上限及違約的風險 ，因
此增加美元短期波動風險。
貿易戰：2026年川普貿易戰從對等關稅全面性課徵轉向法律攻
防，預期2027年貿易政策以各國與美國雙邊貿易協定談判為主
軸，以及針對進口品項實施不同的關稅 (川普8月宣布2027年起
對一系列加拿大汽車產品加徵最高達 50%的關稅)。川普貿易政
策不確定性因素延續 ，但本行認為時序進入 2027年貿易戰全面
性惡化的可能性已降低 ，且企業及投資人已對貿易政策不確定
性有一定程度的認知 ，因此傳導至資產價格的影響性亦將有所
降低。
地緣政治 ：地緣政治風險的擴散效應高於上述財政及關稅 。主
因荷姆茲海峽能源輸出量佔全球 20%，油價上漲對通膨的影響
為2027年美元走勢的主要變數 。2月底事件爆發以來原油遠期
曲線現貨溢價 ，代表短期內市場需求強烈或發生供給中斷 。目
前美伊戰爭從熱戰轉向封鎖荷姆茲海峽 。UAE及部分中東國家
已開始運用地面輸油管線避開該管線 ，但伊朗無人機打擊範圍
遠至含蓋紅海 ，倘若事件面惡化 ，紅海運輸除了葉門胡塞武裝

組織干擾，亦有伊朗無人機攻擊風險。目前美伊談判仍未達成
✓

Thematic Report
重點提示
(3)期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰
爭(3/3)
協議，伊朗拒絕交出高濃縮鈾 ，因此仍無法排除美國運用地面
部隊進行搜索 ，也無法排除伊朗透過遠端無人機攻擊美國在其
他中東國家的軍事或煉油設施 ，美國運用地面部隊恐將地緣政
治風險升級，屆時油價、利率以及通膨預期可能同步向上。
風險變數對2027年美元的影響 ：美國政府債務問題並非全新風
險因子。自1960年以來，美國國會已經累計調整法定債務上限
超過78次。然而，2027年若期中選後國會分裂 ，疊加年底預算
勢必面臨再延後撥款決議 ，屆時政府關門或違約的不確定性因
素將影響經濟成長並且降低聯準會緊縮的可能 。而美國債務上

限再度上調則可能引起信評展望調降的風險 。以美元的角度而
言，有紀律的財政計畫的確可將加強投資人對美元資產的信心 ，
對美元有利 。但短線若因此造成政府關門以及無法在預算達成
協議則對美元短線不利 ，因此定調2027年財政風險因子在分裂
國會環境，對美元而言屬於短空長多。
除了債務風險外 ，貿易戰及美伊戰爭事件的發展最終將反應在
油價及通膨的走勢。從今年油價一度飆升至每桶120美元可見，
短期油價的上漲並未造成短線通膨危機。但不可否認，高油價

若債務上限 、地緣政治 、貿易壁壘等議題未惡化 ，預期美國經
濟偏向溫和成長 ，通膨可望放緩 。觀察中國信託美國景氣動向
指數可見，3個月以來，股市動能略顯放緩，但多數領先指標仍
持續改善 。FactSet 預估2027年標普500企業獲利成長 15%，
且營收成長高達9%，企業獲利面維持高度擴張。樂觀的企業前
景繼續帶動資本支出 ，彭博社統計2027年標普500企業資本支
出年增率仍高達 20%，正向的企業獲利持續傳導到實際經濟 。
2024年以來，實際經濟數據持續改善 ，且今年已回到 0軸以上
擴張區間 。伴隨調查及價格指標持續向上 ，整體景氣動能趨勢

正向。 展望2027年，溫和成長伴隨通膨放緩美元處於盤整偏弱
格局，基本情境內貨幣政策則傾向維持基準利率穩定。

2026/9
義大利民粹政黨執政時期10
年公債殖利率與歐元區利差
30%，歐洲政治生態將進入高度不穩定與兩極化的狀態 。在歐
洲多黨內閣制或雙首長制國家中 ，30%是傳統主流政黨能否順
利執政的關鍵臨界點 。進入2027年，歐盟將有 7國舉行國會選

舉，1月30日德國總統選舉以及4月18日法國總統大選。民粹主
義右翼政黨正在崛起 ，並可能在多個國家大幅增加席次 。每次
選舉結果都將影響各國政治局勢 ，進而反映整個歐洲的政治環
境。然而，右翼民粹主義的崛起對金融市場也並非絕對利空 ，
義大利右翼政黨取得政權後堅持財政紀律 ，向金融市場展現負
責任的治理 ，同時關注移民和文化衝突等社會議題 ，以維繫基
層支持者 。義大利和歐元區 10年期公債利差從義大利右翼政黨
勝選時2.45%至今已收斂至0.83%。此外，許多右翼民粹主義政
黨也緩和了其疑歐立場 ，不再主張退出歐元區或歐盟 。2027年
法國總統大選為首要政治議題。法國極右翼領袖勒龐(Le Pen)與
她所屬的政黨國民聯盟 (RN)近年來調整了政綱 ，已放棄脫歐主
張，並且在財政上淡化激進政策 ，強調財政紀律將遵守歐盟規
範，將財政赤字控制在GDP 3%以下。整體而言，歐洲政治光譜
進入兩極化 ，法國選後議會仍需籌組多數聯盟 ，財政惡化風險
可控，但若極左派梅蘭雄意外勝選，大幅增加政府支出、對富

(6)非美匯市 : 歐洲政治不確定性及日本阻貶為
美元提供底部支撐(5/5)
✓
日圓長期轉為多頭
關鍵因子則將落在
日本政府是否開始
運用政策引導海外
投資資金回流
可視為第一波技術面日圓多方關卡。就基本面而言，政府干預匯
市無法改變貨幣政策與財政政策的路徑。若日本通膨無法帶動日
本央行加速升息，日圓利率水準則無法形成資金流入的誘因。以
日圓長期轉為多頭關鍵因子則將落在日本政府是否開始運用政策
引導海外投資資金回流(日本財務省統計到2025年底，日本在海
外的淨資產達近562兆日圓)，將是長期影響日圓走勢的最有效政
策。
匯市結論: 美元已從2026年初的降息交易轉向受到美伊戰爭、油

價上漲及通膨預期升溫支撐 ，但此波強勢更多來自地緣政治與
高利率環境 ，而非美國基本面出現結構性轉強 。期中選舉後 ，
共和黨失去完全執政優勢 ，國會分裂將限制大規模財政擴張 ，
有助抑制通膨與赤字惡化 ，但也降低進一步刺激經濟成長的動
能。展望2027年，美國仍面臨債務上限 、財政協商、貿易政策
及美伊衝突等風險 ，其中地緣政治與油價走勢仍是左右美元的
重要變數 。外部變數則關注烏俄戰爭是否停戰 、美日聯和干預
日圓與日本2028財年赤字規模 ，以及法國總統大選對歐洲政治
光譜的影響。
整體而言，2027年美元並非進入新一輪強勢多頭週期 ，而是在
高利率支撐下維持區間整理 。2027年美國經濟基準情境穩健 ，
景氣溫和成長 ，通膨從高檔放緩 。若無重大地緣政治升級事件 ，
美元指數預期維持 97-105區間震盪，並呈現「上有壓力、下有
支撐」的盤整格局 。當地緣政治升溫時美元偏強 ，當通膨回落
與利率下降時美元偏弱。

### Page 1

各国経済の ６ ヶ月見通し （10月 ７ 日時点）
Ⅰ . 各国経済の6ヶ月見通し
コメント
① 日本 中東情勢悪化の影響は、 供給面から価格 ・ 所得面へと軸足を移している。 政府 ・ 民間による代替調達の
進展により、 原材料や資源の調達難が生産活動を大きく制約するリスクは後退した一方、 既往の資源
高や原材料費、 物流費などの上昇が川下へ波及し、 家計の実質購買力や企業収益への下押し圧力は
当面残るとみられる。 もっ とも、 こう したコス ト上昇による下押しに対して、 高水準の企業収益が一定の
緩衝材となることに加え、 Ａ Ｉ 関連需要の拡大が半導体や電子部品の輸出 ・ 生産、 設備投資などを押し
上げていることは景気の下支え要因となる。 個人消費は物価高や天候不順の影響から弱さが残るも
のの、 高い賃上げや底堅い雇用環境が支えとなる。 景気は力強さこそ欠くものの、 緩やかな回復基調
を維持する可能性が高い。
② 米国 米経済は堅調さを維持しており、 労働市場も安定推移をたどっている。 米国 ・ イラン間の和平交渉に行

詰まりがみられるなか、 ガソリンをはじめとするエネルギー価格は再び上昇に転じた。 こう した情勢下、
Ｆ Ｒ Ｂ は2026年9月の Ｆ ＯＭＣ において、 25bpの利上げを全会一致で決定した。 今後は トランプ政権の
政策を巡る不確実性の高まりに加え、 サプライチェーンの混乱や保険料負担の増大が経済の抑制要
因になると見込まれる。 一方で、 Ａ Ｉ 需要の拡大や減税効果が下支えとなり、 2026年の米経済は総じて
底堅く推移する見通しである。 以上の状況を踏まえ、 Ｆ Ｒ Ｂ はインフレ目標の達成に向け、 追加利上げあ
るいは高金利水準を維持すると予想される。
③ 欧州 ドイツの歴史的な財政政策の転換、 欧州各国の防衛費拡大、 輸出環境の改善などを受けて、 ユーロ圏
経済は緩やかな回復基調を維持している。 今後もこう した環境が続く とみられるが、 先行きの不透明感
も高まっている。 イラン情勢の緊張長期化を受け、 エネルギー価格が再び上昇に転じており、 企業収益
の圧迫や家計購買力の目減りが避けられない。 インフレを警戒し、 Ｅ Ｃ Ｂ が利上げを続けており、 企業の

資金調達環境が悪化している。 南欧諸国の好調を支えてきた欧州復興基金は、 年末で新規の財政支
援を終了する。 財政再建の遅れや政治環境が不安視されるフランスの国債利回りが上昇しており、 一
部で他の欧州諸国への波及懸念も浮上している。
④   ア ジ ア・
新興国
イラン情勢は依然と して不透明であり、 エネルギー価格が高止まりする可能性が残る。 ただし、 世界的
なＡ Ｉ ・ 半導体需要や関連投資の拡大が、 一部の国で外需や国内投資を押し上げ、 エネルギーインフレの
影響を相殺している。 一方、 エルニーニ ョ 現象による異常気象が食料インフレを招く懸念があり、 生活
必需品を中心とする物価上昇が内需の足かせとなる可能性が高まっている。 Ｆ Ｒ Ｂ の政策運営を巡る懸
念は幾分後退しているが、 経済のフ ァンダメンタルズ （基礎的条件） が脆弱な新興国では、 資金流出に伴
う通貨安が輸入物価を押し上げるリスクもある。 このため、 各国の金融政策運営は一段と難しさを増す
と予想される。 経済構造の違いによって物価や景気への影響は異なり、 各国間で景気の勢いにばらつ

きが生じる可能性には注意が必要である。

## Document 594: 内外経済ウォッチ『アジア・新興国～AI半導体は韓国景気に追い風も､政治の逆風が続く～』(2026年11月号)

Source PDF: all report pdf/内外経済ウォッチ『アジア・新興国～AI半導体は韓国景気に追い風も､政治の逆風が続く～』(2026年11月号).pdf

アジア ・ 新興国
～AI半導体は韓国景気に追い風も､政治の逆風が続く～
韓国経済は、 世界的なＡ Ｉ （人工知能） ・ 半導体関連需要
の拡大が追い風となっている。 韓国は、 DRAMやNAND
型フラッシュメモリ、 広帯域メモリ （Ｈ ＢＭ） などメモリ半導
体の大規模量産で世界 ト ップクラスの競争力を有する。
９ 月の輸出額は前年比＋83.5％となり、 単月ベースの輸出
額は過去最高額を更新した。
韓国経済は、 Ｇ Ｄ Ｐ に占める輸出比率が ４ 割を上回るな
ど、 構造面で外需依存度が相対的に高い。 このため、 Ａ Ｉ ・ 半
導体関連をけん引役にした輸出拡大の動きは景気を押し
上げると期待される。 事実、 中銀は ８ 月の定例会合におい
て、 2026年通年の経済成長率を＋3.3％と ５ 年ぶりの高成
長に上方修正するとともに、 2027年も＋2.9％と高水準で
推移するとの見方を示している。
こう した状況にもかかわらず、 このところの李在明政権
の支持率は低迷している。 最近の世論調査では、 政権支

持率が ３ 割台で推移する一方、 不支持率は ５ 割を上回って
おり、 改善する見通しが立たない状況が続いている。 背景
のひとつには、 不動産価格の高騰が挙げられる。 李政権
は昨年来、 不動産価格高騰への様々な対策を打ち出した
ものの、 実需層の資金調達が困難になる一方、 規制対象
外の地域で不動産価格が上昇するなど 「副作用」 も顕在
化した。 また、 政府は超高額物件を対象とする課税強化、
非居住者に対する増税を目指したものの、 中高所得者層
などからの反発を受けて撤回に追い込まれた。 こう した
不動産対策への不満は、 ６ 月に実施された首都ソウル特
別市長選で与党候補が敗北した一因との見方もある。 そ
の後もソウルを中心に不動産価格の上昇が続いており、
李政権を巡る状況は厳しさを増している。
Ａ Ｉ ・ 半導体需要拡大は韓国景気の追い風に
不動産高騰を理由に、 政権支持率は低迷が続く
旺盛な輸出による貿易黒字の拡大に加え、 中銀のタカ
派姿勢も重なり、 足元のウォン相場は大きく持ち直してい

る。 一方、 主要株価指数 （KOSPI） は、 主要半導体メーカー
の株価動向に左右される形で低迷し、 足元ではピークを ３
割近く下回る。 こう した金融市場の不安定化も、 家計や投
資家の心理を通じて政権への評価に影響している可能性
がある。
さらに、 イラン情勢の悪化によるエネルギーインフレに
加え、 半導体産業の一部では営業利益などの一定割合
（N％） を従業員の成果報酬の原資とする 「N％成果給」 を
導入、 他産業でも導入を求める動きが広がるなど、 賃金
インフレも警戒される。 また、 スーパーエルニーニョの発
生により、 韓国では高温や少雨による悪影響が農業生産
に出るほか、 世界的な干ばつや洪水による食料インフレ
も懸念され、 李政権にと って逆風となる可能性がある。
韓国では、 革新派の支持層が ３ 割弱とされるが、 足元で
は若年層を中心に支持率の低下が加速しており、 支持層
をつなぎとめることが困難になりつつある。 韓国の歴代
政権では、 支持率が低下する局面で対日政策が厳し くな

るケースが散見された。 しかし、 李政権は発足以降、 国益
を重視した 「実利外交」 を掲げており、 現時点でそう した兆
しはみられない。
とはいえ、 李政権の実利外交を巡っては、 与党 ・ 共に民
主党の主流派を中心に反発が根強く、 党内対立の火種と
なる可能性は残る。 現在の良好な日韓関係が今後も続く
か、 韓国国内の情勢を見極める必要性は高まっている。
ウォン相場は持ち直しも、 株価は混乱が続く
良好な日韓関係に影響するか、 注意が必要に

## Document 599: 凱基投顧_2337 旺宏_沈漢軒_20261016

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_2337 旺宏_沈漢軒_20261016.pdf

### Page 1

十月 16, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 記憶體 ‧ 台灣

旺宏 
(2337.TW /2337 TT)

3Q26 法說預覽：利基需求支撐長期獲利  
重要訊息 
旺宏將於 10/27 公告 3Q26 財報並召開法說會 。 
評論及分析  
產出與庫存限制 3Q26 出貨，ASP 提升支撐毛利。3Q26 營收 242.3 億元，
達我們預估的 91.6%。儘管 NOR/SLC NAND/eMMC 報價分別季增約 40-
60%/70- 100%/20 -30%，營收僅季增 26.7%，我們推測主因為：(1) 1H26
將 NOR/ROM 產能轉向 eMMC，造成轉換期間的產出損失； (2) NOR/SLC 
NAND 庫存較低 ；(3) eMMC 良率提升不如預期。上述因素導致 NOR/SLC 
NAND 出貨量季減，eMMC 則僅小幅季增。不過，ASP 上漲仍將帶動毛利率
提升至 71.5%，EPS 預估調整至 6.1 元。 
預估 4Q26 EPS 8.2 元。展望 4Q26，我們預期：(1) SLC NAND 報價續漲
40%；(2) eMMC 漲幅收斂至個位數；(3)旺宏 NOR 報價策略將明顯較華邦
電(2344 TT ，NT$161.5 ，增加持股 )保守，主因產能與庫存較為受限。位元
出貨方面，隨 TLC/MLC 良率持續改善，eMMC 出貨量可望季增 15%；惟
SLC NAND/ NOR 受限於庫存有限、產能未提升，出貨量將大致持平。我們預
估 4Q26 營收達 292.3 億元，季增 20.6%，毛利率提升至 75.6%，修正 EPS
預估至 8.2 元。 
利基需求支撐價格地板，長期獲利具延續性 。展望 2027-28 年，受惠 Edge 
AI 裝置、Boot Drive 及航太國防等重視可靠度的利基應用需求穩健，我們預
期 SLC NAND/NOR 供需仍將緊俏，報價維持高檔；eMMC 則以車用需求最
為穩定。若設備順利進駐，旺宏預計於 4Q27 新增 2.1k 3D NAND 與 4k 12
吋 NOR 產能，並於 1H28 貢獻產出，主要為 支應上述應用需求。我們將
2027 年營收預估修正至 1,446.5 億元，毛利率 77.9%，EPS 42.8 元。儘管
受限於漲幅放緩與產能有限， 短期獲利增速不如原先預期，考量上述利基應
用將支撐 Flash 價格地板，我們仍看好旺宏長期獲利的延續性。  
投資建議  
考量獲利成長低於原先預期，我們將目標本益比下修至 5 倍(相較利基型記
憶體龍頭南亞科(2408 TT ，NT$447.5，增加持股)的 7 倍)，以 2026-27 年
EPS 為評價基礎，目標價 155 元。惟長期獲利仍穩健，且目前評價具吸引
力，維持旺宏「增加持股」評等。  
投資風險  
擴產設備進機時辰延後 、工廠營運與人力面臨瓶頸。
